| **Phase 7A — header quick wins (Permissions-Policy, X-Powered-By, HSTS preload, helper)** | Closes the four header-level gaps surfaced by external scans (OWASP ZAP 2.17, Snyk Security Headers, MDN HTTP Observatory) without touching CSP — that ships in Phase 7B with its own report-only rollout, nonce middleware, and third-party domain inventory. **Pre-fix curl audit (innerzero.com, all responses captured before any code change):** `curl -I` on `/`, `/pricing`, `/login`, `/api/cloud/plans`, `/api/stats`, `/api/feed`, `/favicon.ico`, `/robots.txt`, `/sitemap.xml`, `/og-default.png`, `/banner.png`, `/images/logo.png`, the 404 page, the OPTIONS preflight on `/api/cloud/plans`, the 401 on `/api/cloud/balance`, the 307 on `/account`, the 308 on http→https, the 308 on `www.innerzero.com`, and a static `/_next/static/*` 404 returned: (a) `X-Powered-By: Next.js` leaking on the HTML responses (`/`, `/account` 307) — set on every Next.js response unless `poweredByHeader: false` is configured. (b) Zero `Permissions-Policy` on any path. (c) `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=63072000` on every path that returned headers — value is exactly 2 years but lacks `includeSubDomains` and `preload`. **HSTS source resolved:** the value comes from the **Vercel platform default for the custom domain**, not from this codebase. `next.config.ts` headers() does not list HSTS (verified by reading file pre-change), `vercel.json` has only the cron block (verified — no `headers` key), and the value is identical across dynamic Next routes, static files, and 404s served by the CDN. The Phase 6 build-log row didn't list HSTS because Phase 6 didn't ship it; the platform was emitting it all along. (d) ZAP's "5 paths missing X-Content-Type-Options" was **not reproducible** in the curl sweep — every Next.js-served response above carried `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, and `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` from `next.config.ts:headers()` source `"/:path*"`, including `/api/feed` (uses `new Response(..., { headers: {...} })`), the `/api/cloud/balance` 401 (uses `NextResponse.json` with no explicit headers), and the OPTIONS 204 preflight. The brief's hypothesis that custom-header response constructors bypass `next.config.ts` headers is empirically false in the current Next.js 16.2.1 + Vercel runtime — those headers are merged at the framework layer regardless of how the Response was built. The only response surface that returned **no** security headers was the Vercel-edge `http://innerzero.com/` → 308 redirect to HTTPS, which is generated below the Next.js application layer and is unreachable from `next.config.ts`; after HSTS preload propagates it becomes a no-op for any browser that has visited the site previously. **Implementation:** **(1) `poweredByHeader: false`** added to the root of `nextConfig` in `next.config.ts` — single-line flip, suppresses `X-Powered-By` on every response Next.js emits. Repo grep for the literal `X-Powered-By` returned zero hits before the flip, so nothing depends on the header. **(2) `Permissions-Policy`** added to the `"/:path*"` block in `next.config.ts:headers()` as a `PERMISSIONS_POLICY` const built via `[...].join(", ")` — modern structured-header syntax, comma-separated, parentheses for the allowlist. Block list (`()`): `camera`, `microphone`, `geolocation`, `usb`, `bluetooth`, `serial`, `hid`, `midi`, `accelerometer`, `gyroscope`, `magnetometer`, `ambient-light-sensor`, `payment`, `autoplay`, `clipboard-read`, `clipboard-write`, `display-capture`, `encrypted-media`, `web-share`, `screen-wake-lock`, `idle-detection`, `interest-cohort`, `browsing-topics`, `attribution-reporting`, `run-ad-auction`, `join-ad-interest-group`. Self-only (`(self)`): `fullscreen`, `picture-in-picture`. `payment=()` is correct because Stripe Checkout is invoked via `stripe.checkout.sessions.create` + redirect (verified `mode: "subscription"` / `"payment"` paths in `src/app/api/stripe/checkout/route.ts`; zero `loadStripe`, `@stripe/stripe-js`, `Elements`, or `EmbeddedCheckout` imports under `src/`, so the embedded Payment Request flow is not in use). Older browsers silently drop unrecognised directives (`interest-cohort`, `browsing-topics`, `attribution-reporting`, `run-ad-auction`, `join-ad-interest-group`) — listing them is forward-cover, not a behaviour change today. Constructed via `.join(", ")` rather than a hand-typed string so a stray missing comma can't silently disable the entire header (browsers drop the whole policy on a parse error). **(3) `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains; preload`** added to the same `"/:path*"` block. Two-year max-age matches the Vercel default; `includeSubDomains` and `preload` are the new directives. Per the brief, this phase only **sets the preload-eligible value**; submission to hstspreload.org is deferred to a later phase. The next.config.ts value overrides the bare Vercel default at the application layer, so post-deploy there is one HSTS source-of-truth (this codebase). `vercel.json` was checked for header conflicts: it has none, no removal required. **(4) Defence-in-depth helper:** `src/lib/security-headers.ts` exports `applySecurityHeaders<T extends Response>(response: T): T` — append-only, uses `response.headers.has(key)` before `set` so an existing CORS, Content-Type, Cache-Control, Retry-After, X-Usage-Remaining, X-Provider, or X-* header is never replaced or duplicated, and the Phase 6 inheritance from `next.config.ts` (which the curl audit confirms is working) cannot be fought by us. Returns the same Response so call sites can `return applySecurityHeaders(res)` inline. Header set mirrors the four `next.config.ts` headers (`X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `Permissions-Policy: <same value as 7A.2>`); HSTS deliberately excluded from the helper because HSTS is HTTPS-only metadata that Vercel's edge already adds and the API responses inherit it from `next.config.ts:headers()` — re-adding it from app code creates duplicate-header risk for no benefit. Module shape (default export absent, named function export, no class) matches `rate-limit.ts` and `auth-desktop.ts` per the brief. **Routes that received the helper** (only the three the audit flagged as constructing their own response with a `headers:` literal — every other route returns plain `NextResponse.json(body)` without a `headers:` key and inherits cleanly from `next.config.ts`): `src/app/api/feed/route.ts` (the `new Response(JSON.stringify(feed)..., { headers: { Content-Type, Cache-Control } })` JSON Feed return); `src/app/api/cloud/proxy/route.ts:602-616` (the success path's `NextResponse.json(body, { headers: { X-Usage-Remaining, X-Provider } })`); `src/app/api/stripe/webhook/route.ts:617-631` (the post-signature-verification 429 rate-limit path, which uses `new Response(json, { status: 429, headers: { Content-Type, Retry-After } })`). The webhook **signature-verification path is untouched** (the helper runs only after `stripe.webhooks.constructEvent` succeeds and the rate-limit branch trips); status, body, and the `request_id` idempotency check are header-only-modified by the helper, so Stripe's contract on this route is preserved. `rate-limit.ts:checkRateLimit`'s shared 429 response was deliberately not wrapped, because that helper is consumed by routes that all return `NextResponse.json` for their success path (so they inherit `next.config.ts` cleanly) and the 429 also inherits empirically — wrapping it would touch every rate-limited route indirectly, expanding scope beyond the three audited candidates. Cron routes (`/api/cron/expire-packs`, `/prune-cost-log`, `/reset-usage`) build plain `NextResponse.json(...)` without a `headers:` literal, so they were not flagged and not touched; their `Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}` auth gate is unchanged and Vercel Cron's `Stripe-cares-about-status-not-headers` analogue applies. **Verification (build-time):** `npm run build` clean. Two warnings emitted, both pre-existing — verified by `git stash` + rebuild on main: (a) `turbopack.root` workspace-root inference notice (present since multiple lockfiles were introduced in earlier phases); (b) `The "middleware" file convention is deprecated. Please use "proxy" instead.` (Next.js 16 deprecation notice, present on commit f58affe before this change). Phase 7A introduces zero new warnings. **Verification (post-deploy):** `curl -I` to be re-run on the same surfaces after Vercel deploys; expected on every Next.js response: no `X-Powered-By`, `Permissions-Policy: <full 28-directive value>`, `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains; preload`, and the existing `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`. External rescans (`securityheaders.com/?q=https%3A%2F%2Finnerzero.com%2F` and `developer.mozilla.org/.../observatory/analyze?host=innerzero.com`) to be triggered after deploy; expected: Snyk lifts to A; Observatory lifts from 75/100 toward ~85/100. CSP missing keeps both off the top grade — that is Phase 7B's job and explicitly out of scope here. Smoke test plan post-deploy: load `/`, `/pricing`, `/login` in a normal browser; confirm theme toggle, hero, Discord link, Stripe Checkout redirect (no payment completion), and Turnstile widget render — Turnstile loads in a third-party iframe to `challenges.cloudflare.com` and is the most likely victim of an over-aggressive `Permissions-Policy`; if `/login` fails to render the captcha post-deploy, that is the first place to bisect. **Deviations from the prompt with reasons:** (a) The brief stated as fact that "Next.js does not merge those headers into responses constructed manually as `new NextResponse(..., { headers: {...} })`" — the curl audit found this to be **empirically false in the current Next.js 16.2.1 + Vercel runtime** for every response surface tested. The helper was still created and applied to the three flagged routes per the brief's explicit instruction, on a defence-in-depth basis (the helper is a no-op when inheritance works and a backstop if a future Next.js change breaks it). The "5 paths missing X-Content-Type-Options" reported by ZAP could not be reproduced; the only header-bare response surface located is the Vercel HTTP→HTTPS 308 redirect, which is platform-level and unreachable from `next.config.ts` — preload propagation is the practical mitigation there. (b) `picture-in-picture=(self)` will block YouTube's PiP control inside the `VideoEmbed` iframe, which loads `youtube-nocookie.com` cross-origin and includes `picture-in-picture` in its iframe `allow=` attribute. Browsers' default for PiP is to allow cross-origin iframes that opt in, so this header tightens behaviour from "iframe gets PiP" to "iframe does not get PiP" — accepted as a minor UX regression on the marketing video, matches the brief's "Allow on self only" intent. The fullscreen change is a no-op because the iframe's `allow` attribute does not currently request fullscreen. (c) `rate-limit.ts:checkRateLimit`'s 429 response was not wrapped (see "Routes that received the helper" above) — narrower scope than a literal "every constructor with headers" reading, justified by the audit showing inheritance covers it. **Constraint compliance:** zero new packages; one new file (`src/lib/security-headers.ts`, 50 lines, named export only); three route files modified (each with one import line + one wrapper call); `next.config.ts` modified (poweredByHeader flag, Permissions-Policy const, three header rows on `/:path*`); `vercel.json` untouched; existing CORS block on `/api/cloud/:path*` and existing favicon Cache-Control block both preserved verbatim; no copy or markup changes; no Tailwind, theme, or component changes; em-dash audit on the new helper file returned zero hits. **PROJECT_MAP regen** triggered because `src/lib/security-headers.ts` is a new file under `src/lib/` per the maintenance rule. | COMPLETE 2026-04-27 |
| **Phase 7B-1 — Content-Security-Policy report-only rollout + Permissions-Policy comma-only fix** | Lays the observation phase for full CSP enforcement (Phase 7B-2) and closes the Phase 7A regression Snyk flagged on the Permissions-Policy directive list separator. **Permissions-Policy regression:** the Phase 7A const used `[...].join(", ")` (comma + OWS) which Snyk's parser rejects with `" attribution-reporting"` (leading space) as an invalid directive name even though RFC 8941 / Structured Fields permit OWS after a comma. Fixed in `next.config.ts` and `src/lib/security-headers.ts` to `.join(",")` — comma only, no whitespace anywhere in the list value. Verified post-deploy: `curl -I https://innerzero.com/ | grep -i permissions-policy` parses to 28 stripped tokens with `directive == directive.strip()` for every entry. **Theme-flash hash:** `'sha256-f7LAjRiK+uoAyu7rUwSbVvtnehpB2z0d+hr4fBMjsds='` covers the 349-byte body of the inline `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` script in `src/app/layout.tsx` (the `(function(){var theme=localStorage.getItem('theme');...})()` IIFE that runs before paint to prevent FOUC during theme detection). Hash regenerated by SHA-256 over the live rendered HTML byte-for-byte (Python `hashlib.sha256` of the template-literal body, base64 of the digest); the rendered string matches the source string exactly so the hash carries forward without React/JSX whitespace mangling. Hash regen instructions live in a comment block in `next.config.ts` and are mirrored in `src/lib/security-headers.ts` so any future Claude Code edit to the theme script will hit the trap (a one-byte change invalidates the hash and breaks the theme on first paint after Phase 7B-2 enforce). Build-time hash automation explicitly deferred. **Audit findings (each justifies a directive entry):** **Inline content** — `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` grep across `src/` returned exactly the layout.tsx theme-flash plus the JsonLd component (which emits `application/ld+json`, non-executable per CSP3 spec — covered by browser exemption, not by directive). MDX blog posts (25 files in `src/content/blog/`) audited for `<script`, `onclick=`, `onerror=`, `onload=`, `javascript:` — zero hits. **Third-party hosts (each cross-referenced from grep + `curl -sL <page> \| grep -oE 'https?://[^\"\' ]+'`):** **Cloudflare Turnstile** at `https://challenges.cloudflare.com` — script (`/turnstile/v0/api.js?render=explicit`, see `src/components/ui/Turnstile.tsx:5`), challenge iframe (cross-origin), verify callbacks via fetch. Consumed by `LoginForm`, `RegisterForm`, `ForgotPasswordForm`, `ResetPasswordForm`, and `NewsletterSignup` — the last lazy-mounts the widget only after first focus/keystroke on the email input. → `script-src`, `frame-src`, `connect-src`. **Vercel Analytics** — production loads `/_vercel/insights/script.js` same-origin and beacons to `/_vercel/insights/view` same-origin (verified via `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` on the live `/about` page); the `@vercel/analytics` package conditionally falls back to `https://va.vercel-scripts.com/v1/script.debug.js` on debug/preview builds (verified by reading `node_modules/@vercel/analytics/dist/index.mjs`). `va.vercel-scripts.com` listed in `script-src` and `connect-src` as defensive cover for preview deploys; production is fully covered by `'self'`. **Supabase** at `https://chdsbjydwswtshjflkva.supabase.co` (https REST + wss Realtime) — derived from `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` at config evaluation time so a project-URL change automatically updates both directives. → `connect-src` https + wss. **YouTube facade** — iframe at `https://www.youtube-nocookie.com` (`src/components/sections/VideoEmbed.tsx:36`), thumbnail at `https://i.ytimg.com` (`maxresdefault.jpg`). Iframe → `frame-src`. Thumbnail → covered by the `img-src https:` wildcard, no explicit entry. **Formspree** at `https://formspree.io` — `src/components/sections/ContactForm.tsx:21` POSTs via `fetch` rather than form `action=`, so → `connect-src`, NOT `form-action`. **Stripe** — confirmed redirect-only by re-grepping in this commit: `src/components/sections/PricingSection.tsx:113` does `window.location.href = data.url` after fetching `/api/stripe/checkout` (which returns the Stripe Checkout session URL); zero `loadStripe`, `@stripe/stripe-js`, `js.stripe.com`, `Stripe.redirectToCheckout`, `Elements`, or `EmbeddedCheckout` in `src/`. Top-level navigation is not gated by CSP, so Stripe needs no directive entry. **Discord, GitHub, X, Instagram, LinkedIn, Ko-fi, ICO, Summers Solutions, ollama.com, schema.org, jsonfeed.org, w3.org, validator.w3.org** — all link `href` targets that never load resources on our pages. Confirmed by curl-extracting hosts from `/`, `/pricing`, `/login`, `/blog/innerzero-is-live`, `/account` and cross-referencing against the rendered DOM — none appear as resource loads, only as anchor href values. None added to CSP. **Fonts** — `next/font/google` for Inter is self-hosted at build time (Next.js bakes the font binaries into `_next/static/media/`); confirmed no `fonts.googleapis.com` or `fonts.gstatic.com` references in any rendered page. → `font-src 'self' data:` is sufficient. **Images** — `img-src 'self' data: blob: https:` deliberately broad. Marketing site has YouTube thumbnails, blog inline images, and OG previews from many origins; images cannot execute. **Styles** — `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'` is a known accepted compromise. Tailwind v4 emits style attributes for some utilities and several components have `style={...}` props (Hero.tsx 2 hits at the hero gradient, CloudUsageCard.tsx 1 hit at the usage progress bar). Hash- or nonce-based style locking would require a refactor outside this phase; XSS via style is materially narrower than via script. **CSP directives shipped (joined with `"; "` per CSP grammar):** `default-src 'self'`; `script-src 'self' 'sha256-f7LAjRiK+...' https://challenges.cloudflare.com https://va.vercel-scripts.com`; `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`; `img-src 'self' data: blob: https:`; `font-src 'self' data:`; `connect-src 'self' https://chdsbjydwswtshjflkva.supabase.co wss://chdsbjydwswtshjflkva.supabase.co https://challenges.cloudflare.com https://formspree.io https://va.vercel-scripts.com`; `frame-src https://challenges.cloudflare.com https://www.youtube-nocookie.com`; `frame-ancestors 'none'` (replaces and supersedes `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, kept side by side for browser-version cover); `form-action 'self'`; `base-uri 'self'`; `object-src 'none'`; `upgrade-insecure-requests`; `report-uri /api/csp-report`; `report-to csp-endpoint`. **Header type:** `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` exclusively — no `Content-Security-Policy` (without `-Report-Only` suffix) header is emitted; verified by `curl -I` on `/`, `/pricing`, `/login`, `/account`, `/api/stats`, `/blog/innerzero-is-live` — `grep -ic "^content-security-policy:"` returns 0 on every path, only the Report-Only variant present. `Report-To` header value `{"group":"csp-endpoint","max_age":10800,"endpoints":[{"url":"/api/csp-report"}]}` — JSON valid; `max_age=10800` (3h) so the browser doesn't cache the endpoint past the smoke window. Both `report-uri` (legacy, Firefox preferred until recently) and `report-to` (modern Chrome/Edge) specified for browser-version cover. **CSP report endpoint:** new `src/app/api/csp-report/route.ts`. POST only, returns 204 No Content, accepts `application/csp-report` (legacy single-report wrapper `{"csp-report":{...}}`) and `application/reports+json` (Reporting API array form), logs a single structured JSON line per report to stdout (`{event:"csp_report", ts, content_type, payload}`) so Vercel runtime logs filter cleanly. Zero persistence, zero rate-limit, zero CORS — same-origin POST from the browser, short-lived observation tool. Tested both content types post-deploy via curl: both return 204. **API route helper extension:** `src/lib/security-headers.ts` extended to also append `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` and `Report-To` when wrapping a custom-header response. Directive set duplicated rather than imported — `next.config.ts` runs in the Node config phase and this file runs inside route handlers, so cross-runtime imports are avoided. Both files cross-reference each other in maintenance comments. The Phase 7A append-only contract is preserved: `if (!response.headers.has(key))` before `set` so existing `next.config.ts` inheritance is never duplicated (verified empirically — Phase 7A audit established that Next.js 16.2.1 on Vercel inherits headers into manually-constructed `NextResponse` objects, see permanent architecture note below). Three flagged routes still wrap (api/feed, api/cloud/proxy success path, api/stripe/webhook 429 branch) — none changed in this phase, only the helper they call gained the two new headers. **Verification (post-deploy curl):** `Permissions-Policy` → 28 directives, zero whitespace; `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` present and parses on every path tested (`/`, `/pricing`, `/login`, `/account` 307, `/api/stats`, `/blog/innerzero-is-live`); `Report-To` parses as valid JSON; HSTS, X-Powered-By suppression, X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options, Referrer-Policy all retained from Phase 7A. **Smoke test (Playwright on the live site, fresh window, 6 pages traversed including `/`, `/about`, `/blog/innerzero-is-live`, `/login`):** captured all `securitypolicyviolation` events plus console messages. **Confirmed working under CSP:** theme-flash IIFE (hash matches), Vercel Analytics script + beacons (`/_vercel/insights/script.js` + `/_vercel/insights/view`, both same-origin), VideoEmbed YouTube iframe on `/about` (`https://www.youtube-nocookie.com/embed/...` loaded after click-to-play), YouTube thumbnail (i.ytimg.com via `img-src https:`), Turnstile widget script load on `/login` (Cloudflare's bot-detection then 401'd Playwright's challenge attempt — which is Cloudflare's defence-in-depth on automation tools, not a CSP issue; real users with no automation flags clear the challenge normally), JSON-LD blocks (non-executable, exempt). **Real CSP violations observed:** **(1) Next.js App Router streaming hydration scripts.** Each page emits ~18-31 inline `<script>` tags carrying `self.__next_f.push(...)` chunks for the server-streamed React payload. Each is a unique inline script body with a unique SHA-256 hash and unique content per request (verified by inspecting 3 distinct pages — `/`, `/about`, `/blog/innerzero-is-live` — with 31, 18, 18 streaming scripts respectively). All fire `securitypolicyviolation` events under our `script-src 'self' <theme-hash> challenges.cloudflare.com va.vercel-scripts.com`. The brief's premise — "the one inline script" — overlooked Next.js streaming. **This is a real production-resource violation that blocks Phase 7B-2 from flipping to enforce as currently written.** Phase 7B-2 must pick one of: (a) accept `'unsafe-inline'` for `script-src` (the easy path, nullifies most of the `script-src` value); (b) wire nonces via `middleware.ts` and switch to `'strict-dynamic'` (the brief explicitly forbade middleware and `'strict-dynamic'` — that constraint will need revisiting); (c) compute every streaming hash at build time and bake them into a static CSP (impractical — content varies per request because the streaming payload is per-page); (d) refactor away from App Router streaming (massive scope, not justified by header hygiene alone). The Phase 7B-2 prompt should either lift one of the three forbidden mitigations or accept the constraint that App Router streaming makes a strict static CSP unreachable. **(2) `upgrade-insecure-requests` ignored in report-only.** Browsers (Chrome) console-log `[ERROR] The Content Security Policy directive 'upgrade-insecure-requests' is ignored when delivered in a report-only policy.` per CSP3 spec — the directive has no report semantic. Cosmetic in 7B-1; the directive activates correctly when 7B-2 flips the header name. **(3) Permissions-Policy `ambient-light-sensor` unrecognized in modern Chrome.** Chrome dropped the directive name in version 73 (2019). Older browsers (and Firefox until recently) still recognise it; the entry is forward/backward compatibility cover, not a bug. The brief's Phase 7A directive list explicitly included it; left in place. **(4) Permissions-Policy `picture-in-picture` blocks YouTube iframe PiP control.** Same trade-off documented in Phase 7A — `picture-in-picture=(self)` denies the cross-origin YouTube iframe its PiP capability. Console reports `Permissions policy violation: picture-in-picture is not allowed in this document` from inside the YouTube player JS. Iframe loads, video plays, only PiP control is unavailable. Accepted minor UX regression. **(5) Permissions-Policy `accelerated-2d-canvas` and `xr-spatial-tracking` warnings.** Coming from the iframe `allow="..."` attributes (VideoEmbed's iframe requests `accelerated-2d-canvas`; Turnstile's iframe requests `xr-spatial-tracking`). Chrome doesn't recognise `accelerated-2d-canvas` and disallows `xr-spatial-tracking` for cross-origin iframes by default. The iframe `allow` attributes predate this phase and are unchanged. **(6) Inside the Turnstile iframe (out of scope).** `TrustedHTML`/`TrustedScript`/`TrustedScriptURL` errors and a `script-src 'nonce-...' 'unsafe-eval'` violation at `about:srcdoc` — these are Cloudflare's own internal CSP applied inside their challenge iframe, unrelated to our policy. Filtered out. **Browser-extension noise filter:** none captured in this run because Playwright runs without extensions. Real users will trigger CSP reports for `chrome-extension://*` and `moz-extension://*` URIs from password managers, ad blockers, and accessibility extensions; the report endpoint logs these but they are noise to be filtered when the smoke window's reports are reviewed. **External rescans deliberately skipped this phase:** `securityheaders.com` does not grade `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`; same for MDN Observatory. Both will lift only after Phase 7B-2 flips to enforce, and the Phase 7B-2 row will record those numbers. This is an expected non-result, documented here to prevent it being read as a regression. **What Phase 7B-2 still needs to do:** (1) decide on the streaming-script mitigation (nonces/middleware vs unsafe-inline vs other), (2) flip header name from `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` to `Content-Security-Policy`, (3) decide whether `upgrade-insecure-requests` should remain in enforce mode (it activates automatically once the header name flips), (4) decide whether `va.vercel-scripts.com` belongs in production after the smoke confirmed it never loads (current call: keep as defensive cover), (5) trigger Snyk and Observatory rescans and record the new grades. **PROJECT_MAP regen** triggered because `src/app/api/csp-report/route.ts` is a new file. | COMPLETE 2026-04-27 |
| **Phase 7B-2 — CSP enforce flip via middleware nonce + strict-dynamic, Picture-in-Picture regression fix** | Closes the Phase 7B-1 observation phase by switching `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` to `Content-Security-Policy` with no `'unsafe-inline'` in `script-src`. The mechanism is per-request nonces issued by `src/middleware.ts` with `'strict-dynamic'`, which is the only static-CSP-compatible way to cover the 18-31 per-page Next.js App Router streaming inline scripts (`self.__next_f.push(...)`) that gated 7B-2. The brief explicitly accepted the SSG → Edge SSR trade-off this phase forces; HTML edge cache is gone for marketing pages, asset CDN cache is unaffected. **Picture-in-Picture regression fix (sequenced first in the diff so it is independently revertable):** Phase 7B-1 smoke surfaced `Permissions policy violation: picture-in-picture is not allowed in this document` from inside the YouTube iframe at `/about`. Diagnosis was neither pure case (a) `picture-in-picture=()` nor pure case (b) missing iframe `allow=` — the Phase 7A directive was already `(self)` and `src/components/sections/VideoEmbed.tsx:65` already requested `picture-in-picture` in its iframe `allow=` attribute (and `allowFullScreen` for fullscreen). The actual blocker was that `(self)` in W3C Permissions Policy only delegates to same-origin documents; a cross-origin iframe to `https://www.youtube-nocookie.com` is not in the parent allowlist and therefore is denied even when its `allow=` attribute requests the feature. **Fix:** in both `next.config.ts` and `src/lib/security-headers.ts` change `picture-in-picture=(self)` → `picture-in-picture=(self "https://www.youtube-nocookie.com")` and `fullscreen=(self)` → `fullscreen=(self "https://www.youtube-nocookie.com")`. Inner-allowlist items separate by single space per the W3C spec; the outer directive separator stays `,` with no whitespace per the Phase 7B-1 Snyk fix. Verified post-deploy via `document.featurePolicy.allowedFeatures().includes('picture-in-picture') === true` on `/about` and zero PiP-related console errors on innerzero.com pages (the inner-iframe Cloudflare/YouTube errors that remain are inside their own iframes and out of scope per the phase definition). **CSP architectural shift:** moved `Content-Security-Policy` and `Report-To` OUT of `next.config.ts:headers()` and INTO `src/middleware.ts`. `next.config.ts:headers()` retains the five static headers (`X-Content-Type-Options`, `X-Frame-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, `Permissions-Policy`, `Strict-Transport-Security`) applied to `'/(.*)'` so they reach static asset responses too. `src/lib/security-headers.ts::applySecurityHeaders` was correspondingly simplified to no longer write `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` or `Report-To` — a static helper cannot inject per-request nonces, and shipping a nonceless CSP from a helper would override the middleware-issued one. The helper now writes only the five static headers (HSTS added to keep the helper consistent with the `next.config.ts:headers()` set). Three routes that wrap their custom responses (api/feed, api/cloud/proxy success path, api/stripe/webhook 429) gain the same simplification automatically through the helper. **Middleware design** (`src/middleware.ts`): per request — (1) generate a 128-bit cryptographically random nonce via `crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(16))` then base64-encode via `btoa(String.fromCharCode(…))`; this is Edge-runtime safe (Node's `crypto` module is unavailable in Edge, `Buffer` is sometimes available but the Web-API path is portable). (2) Build the CSP directive set with `script-src 'self' 'nonce-X' 'strict-dynamic' 'sha256-f7LAjRiK+...'` — the host allowlists from Phase 7B-1 (`https://challenges.cloudflare.com`, `https://va.vercel-scripts.com`) are dropped from `script-src` because CSP3-compliant browsers IGNORE host-source allowlists in `script-src` when `'strict-dynamic'` is present, and the nonce-trusted Next.js loader propagates trust to dynamically-injected scripts (Turnstile, Vercel Analytics) via the documented strict-dynamic chain. The same hosts remain in `connect-src` and `frame-src`, where `'strict-dynamic'` does NOT apply. The theme-flash SHA-256 hash stays in `script-src` as defence-in-depth: in CSP3 browsers the inline script loads via the auto-applied nonce attribute and the hash is unused; on a hypothetical CSP2-only browser the hash is the fallback. (3) Forward both `x-nonce` and the CSP value on the request via `extraRequestHeaders` to `updateSession`, which now passes them through every `NextResponse.next({ request: { headers: … } })` call — including the cookie-rotation path that Supabase's `setAll` callback triggers when the auth token refreshes. Rebuilding the forwarded headers inside the closure is critical because Supabase's cookie set mutates `request.headers` in place; without the rebuild, the forwarded headers would carry stale cookie state. Per the Vercel `/vercel/next.js` docs, Next.js parses the request's CSP header to extract the nonce and auto-applies the `nonce` attribute to: framework scripts (React + Next.js runtime), page bundles, streaming hydration `<script>` tags, and `<Script>` components rendered with the `nonce` prop. (4) Set `Content-Security-Policy` and `Report-To` on the outbound response so the browser actually enforces. /api/* paths short-circuit past the nonce work — they return JSON, not HTML, and don't need CSP; saves the random-bytes + base64 cost on every cloud-proxy and licence call. (5) Matcher extended to also exclude `llms*.txt`, `feed.xml`, `changelog.xml` so those XML/text feeds keep CDN-cached static delivery. **Root layout dynamic opt-in** (`src/app/layout.tsx`): added `import { headers } from 'next/headers'` and `const nonce = (await headers()).get('x-nonce') ?? undefined` at the top of the async `RootLayout`. The Vercel docs explicitly state nonce-based CSP requires dynamic rendering because Next.js applies the nonce during SSR — a static page rendered at build time has no per-request headers to read. Pre-flip build output had every marketing page as `○ (Static)`; reading `headers()` once at layout level cascades dynamic to every child page. The retrieved nonce is also passed to the theme-flash `<script nonce={nonce}>` so the script gets a second valid path on top of the hash. **Routes that transitioned from static (○) to dynamic (ƒ)** (verified by post-build diff, `npm run build` route table): `/`, `/_not-found`, `/about`, `/blog`, `/blog/[slug]` (was SSG ● with generateStaticParams), `/changelog`, `/contact`, `/download`, `/features`, `/for/developers`, `/for/offline-work`, `/for/researchers`, `/for/students`, `/for/writers`, `/forgot-password`, `/login`, `/models`, `/pricing`, `/privacy`, `/register`, `/reset-password`, `/terms`, `/waitlist`, `/what-is-local-ai` — 24 routes total. **Routes that remained static (○)**: `/api/feed`, `/api/stats`, `/changelog.xml`, `/feed.xml` — they don't flow through the root layout, the matcher excludes them anyway. /api/* function routes were already `ƒ` and continue to be. **Verification (build):** `npm run build` clean. The only warnings are the two pre-existing ones (`turbopack.root` workspace-root inference + `"middleware" file convention is deprecated; use "proxy"` Next.js 16 deprecation notice). Phase 7B-2 introduces zero new warnings. **Verification (post-deploy curl):** on `/`, `/pricing`, `/login`, `/account` (307 to /login), `/api/cloud/plans`, `/blog/innerzero-is-live` — `Content-Security-Policy` header present with a unique 128-bit nonce, `'strict-dynamic'`, the SHA-256 hash, the full directive set; `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` ABSENT (regression check); `Report-To` parses; the five static headers retained from Phase 7A. Two consecutive curls on `/` returned different nonces (`nonce-eUZk8Li8MPaxSNnqACHJgQ==` then `nonce-r14J1SmjzIDJxSQ2vXUgsQ==`) confirming per-request randomness and no edge caching of the dynamic response. On static-asset paths (`/favicon.ico`, `/robots.txt`, `/og-default.png`) and the cron-static API routes (`/api/stats`) — no CSP, no Report-To, but the five static headers all present (the `next.config.ts:headers()` `'/(.*)'` block reaches them; middleware does not). Snyk's leading-space Permissions-Policy regression remains fixed (28 directives, zero whitespace). **Verification (browser smoke, Playwright on the live site):** **(/)** 28 inline scripts, ALL 28 nonce-tagged with the same per-request nonce; `inlineWithoutNonce: 0`. Vercel Analytics resources confirmed loaded via `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')`: `https://innerzero.com/_vercel/insights/script.js` + `https://innerzero.com/_vercel/insights/view`. Zero console messages. **(/pricing)** zero console messages. **(/login)** Turnstile script loaded successfully via network (`https://challenges.cloudflare.com/turnstile/v0/api.js?render=explicit`), confirmed via `performance.getEntriesByType` — `'strict-dynamic'` propagation works because the Turnstile loader is injected by the Turnstile.tsx component code, which itself runs as part of the nonce-trusted Next.js runtime. The script element does not have a nonce attribute (Cloudflare's loader doesn't add one), but is permitted because the trusted React tree was the parser-blocking source. The 16 console errors observed are all `https://challenges.cloudflare.com/cdn-cgi/challenge-platform/...` and `about:srcdoc` — inside Cloudflare's own iframe, governed by their own CSP and Trusted Types policy; identical pattern to Phase 7B-1, out of scope. The `Failed to load resource: 401` from `cdn-cgi/pat/...` is Cloudflare's bot detection rejecting the Playwright automation profile, not a CSP issue (real-browser users without automation flags clear the challenge normally). **(/about + VideoEmbed)** click-to-play triggered, iframe mounted at `https://www.youtube-nocookie.com/embed/...`, iframe `allow` retained `picture-in-picture` and `allowFullScreen`, parent `document.featurePolicy.allowedFeatures().includes('picture-in-picture') === true` confirms the PiP regression is fixed at the doc policy level. The YouTube player JS no longer logs the `picture-in-picture is not allowed in this document` violation that was the Phase 7B-1 finding. The remaining warning, `Unrecognized feature: 'accelerated-2d-canvas'`, comes from the iframe `allow=` attribute and is a Chrome warning (the directive name was deprecated); pre-existing, not introduced by this phase. **(/blog/innerzero-is-live)** 15 inline scripts, all 15 nonce-tagged; zero console messages; MDX renders. **External rescans (post-deploy):** Snyk securityheaders.com lifted to **A+** (the `class="score_lightgreen"><span>A+</span>` element confirms it server-side; six headers all green: Content-Security-Policy, Permissions-Policy, Referrer-Policy, Strict-Transport-Security, X-Content-Type-Options, X-Frame-Options). Mozilla Observatory lifted to **A+, score 110/100, tests_passed 10/10, tests_failed 0** (verified via the official `https://observatory-api.mdn.mozilla.net/api/v2/scan` POST API; scan id 93641715, algorithm_version 5). The 110 score reflects Observatory's bonus points for going beyond the baseline tests. CSP test now passes — was -25 in Phase 7B-1 — driving most of the lift from 75 to 110 (35-point swing on CSP plus 0 deductions on the fully-set Permissions-Policy / HSTS-with-preload / cookies / sub-resource integrity etc.). **PageSpeed before/after:** rate-limited by Google's unauthenticated daily quota at the time of this commit (`pagespeedonline.googleapis.com` returned 429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED). Manual run via `https://pagespeed.web.dev/analysis?url=https%3A%2F%2Finnerzero.com%2F` is the next sanity check the user can run — expected to show LCP regression of 30-80ms versus a hypothetical pre-flip baseline (the standard Edge-SSR overhead) and remain in the "Good" CrUX bucket; if LCP regressed >200ms the matcher likely caught an asset path. Document in Phase 7C entry once measured. **Roll-back plan:** if a critical-path regression appears post-deploy that is NOT a Cloudflare/YouTube iframe-internal noise pattern, the immediate revert is one word in `src/middleware.ts`: change the `Content-Security-Policy` header name in the `response.headers.set(...)` call to `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`. That re-enters observation mode without touching the matcher, the nonce work, the helper, or the architecture. **Constraint compliance:** zero new packages; zero new files (csp-report endpoint from Phase 7B-1 carries forward unchanged); five existing files modified (`next.config.ts`, `src/middleware.ts`, `src/lib/supabase/middleware.ts`, `src/app/layout.tsx`, `src/lib/security-headers.ts`); existing CORS block on `/api/cloud/:path*` and favicon Cache-Control block both preserved verbatim; no copy or markup changes; no Tailwind, theme, or component changes; the YouTube iframe `allow=` attribute and `allowFullScreen` were already correct, only the parent's Permissions-Policy widened. **PROJECT_MAP regen NOT triggered:** all five edits were to existing files; no new file under `src/` for the generator to pick up. **What Phase 7C+ still needs:** (1) hstspreload.org submission once subdomain audit is done, (2) optional Trusted Types CSP3 directive, (3) optional Subresource Integrity rollout for third-party scripts, (4) re-evaluate `va.vercel-scripts.com` / `formspree.io` / `challenges.cloudflare.com` host allowlists in `connect-src` and `frame-src` for tightening, (5) review the still-present `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` on static asset responses (Vercel platform default for served static files; the Phase 7A CORS block on `/api/cloud/:path*` is the only place we set CORS ourselves). **Deviations from the prompt with reasons:** (a) brief listed PiP fix as cases (a)/(b)/(c) but the actual diagnosis was neither — iframe `allow=` was already correct AND parent was already `(self)`, but `(self)` does not delegate cross-origin per W3C spec; the fix is to expand the parent allowlist to include the iframe's origin explicitly. Documented above. (b) PageSpeed before/after numbers are not in this row because the unauthenticated PageSpeed API was rate-limited at commit time; the user will do the manual check via web UI. (c) Audit step 7 in the brief implied the fix would be a one-liner choice between (a) widening allowlist to `(self)` or (b) adding iframe attribute; reality required widening allowlist to `(self "https://www.youtube-nocookie.com")` and applying the same widening to `fullscreen=` because the iframe also requests fullscreen via `allowFullScreen`. The fix landed in two directives, not one, and applies to both files that hold the Permissions-Policy const (`next.config.ts` and `src/lib/security-headers.ts`) so the middleware-bypassing helper path also carries the corrected policy. | COMPLETE 2026-04-27 |
| **Phase 7C — Lighthouse SEO descriptive-link fix + Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy** | Closes the two trivial Lighthouse gaps left after Phase 7B-2 in a single small commit. **Pre-flip baseline (PageSpeed Insights desktop, Phase 7B-2 deploy):** Performance 98, Accessibility 91, Best Practices 100, SEO 92. The two flagged gaps were: (a) SEO 92 — `Links do not have descriptive text` against the homepage hero secondary CTA visible text "Learn more" pointing to `/features`; (b) Best Practices 100 (already perfect) but with the unscored advisory `Ensure proper origin isolation with COOP` because no `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy` header was emitted. Out of scope (deliberate deferrals): the colour/contrast accessibility findings driving Accessibility 91, unused-JavaScript reduction, legacy-JavaScript polyfill removal, Trusted Types CSP3 directive, Subresource Integrity. **Audit findings:** the "Learn more" string appears exactly once in the entire repo — `src/lib/constants.ts:44` as `secondaryCta: "Learn more"` — and is consumed by `src/components/sections/Hero.tsx:62` as `{HERO.secondaryCta}`. Hero.tsx itself has no hardcoded fallback. No tests, e2e harness, integration suite, schema.org markup, sitemap entry, or translation file references the string (verified by repo-wide grep on `\"Learn more\"` / `'Learn more'` / `>Learn more<`; the only hit is the constants line itself; no `__tests__/` `tests/` or `e2e/` directories exist on this site). The edit therefore lives in `constants.ts` only, in keeping with the established pattern that hero copy flows through the `HERO` constant. **Implementation 1 — descriptive link copy:** `src/lib/constants.ts:44` flipped from `secondaryCta: "Learn more"` to `secondaryCta: "See features"`. Selection rationale: three words, action-verb-led, descriptive of the destination route `/features`, matches the marketing-page tone elsewhere on the site. Considered and rejected — `"Learn about InnerZero"` (destination is a feature breakdown, not the about page; `/about` is a separate route, so this would mismatch); `"Explore features"` (adequate but "Explore" is overused marketing filler); `"View features"` (adequate but slightly more passive than `"See features"`). **Implementation 2 — Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy header:** `next.config.ts:headers()` adds `{ key: "Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy", value: "same-origin" }` to the `'/:path*'` block so the header reaches every response Next.js emits, including static-asset responses that bypass middleware. Value selection: `same-origin` is the strictest defensible value — it strips the `window.opener` reference for any cross-origin window opened from this site and refuses to expose this site's window handle to any cross-origin opener. Considered and rejected — `same-origin-allow-popups` (would only be needed if a feature on the site opens a popup AND needs to communicate back; this site does not; redirect-only Stripe Checkout, iframe-only Turnstile, redirect-only Supabase auth callback all confirmed in Phase 7B-1's audit and re-confirmed for this commit by grep on `window.open`/`postMessage` returning zero application-code hits); `unsafe-none` (the default opt-out — would not address the advisory). The header lives in `next.config.ts` rather than `src/middleware.ts` because the value is identical for every request and does not need per-request computation; piling it onto middleware would muddy the Phase 7B-2 architectural split (next.config.ts owns static, middleware owns CSP+nonce). **Implementation 3 — helper extension:** `src/lib/security-headers.ts` `SECURITY_HEADERS` array gains `["Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy", "same-origin"]` (and a named `COOP_VALUE` const for clarity). Reasoning: the helper covers the three custom-response routes (`api/feed`, `api/cloud/proxy`, `api/stripe/webhook`); they return non-HTML payloads so COOP is largely cosmetic on them, but consistency matters — if a future helper consumer returns HTML, COOP will already be in place. Append-only contract preserved (`if (!response.headers.has(key))` before `set`), so the next.config.ts inheritance is never duplicated. The Stripe webhook signature-verification path is unchanged (helper runs only on the post-signature-verification 429 branch). **Verification (build):** `npm run build` clean. The only warnings are the two pre-existing ones (`turbopack.root` workspace-root inference + `"middleware" file convention is deprecated; use "proxy"` Next.js 16 deprecation notice). Phase 7C introduces zero new warnings. The route classification is unchanged from Phase 7B-2 — 24 marketing/auth/blog routes remain `ƒ (Dynamic)`, the four XML/feed routes (`/api/feed`, `/api/stats`, `/changelog.xml`, `/feed.xml`) remain `○ (Static)`. **Verification (post-deploy curl):** **(/)** all seven security headers present: `Content-Security-Policy` (with fresh per-request nonce, e.g. `nonce-nhgVGolEFShJ8DOkkcVc0w==`), `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`, `Permissions-Policy` (28 directives, zero whitespace), `Referrer-Policy`, `Strict-Transport-Security`, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `X-Frame-Options`. **(/api/feed)** `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin` confirmed — verifies the helper extension landed and the JSON Feed response now ships COOP alongside its `Content-Type: application/feed+json` and `Cache-Control` headers. **(/favicon.ico static asset)** COOP present, confirms the `/:path*` `next.config.ts:headers()` block reaches static assets that bypass middleware. **(/api/stats static API route)** COOP present. **HTML body fetch on `/`** confirms `"See features"` rendered, zero `"Learn more"` matches anywhere on the homepage. **Verification (browser smoke, Playwright on the live site):** zero console messages on `/` (no new CSP, COOP, or other warnings versus the Phase 7B-2 baseline — `same-origin` COOP can in rare cases trigger console warnings if a script attempts to access `window.opener` cross-origin, but there are none on this site). The hero secondary CTA is a single anchor with `textContent === "See features"` and `href="/features"`; clicking it navigates to `/features` (page-title verification: `Features | InnerZero: Private AI Assistant`). Repo-wide DOM grep on the rendered homepage HTML finds zero `\bLearn more\b` matches. **External rescans (post-deploy):** Snyk securityheaders.com still **A+** (six original headers all green: Content-Security-Policy, Permissions-Policy, Referrer-Policy, Strict-Transport-Security, X-Content-Type-Options, X-Frame-Options). COOP is not in Snyk's headline grade rubric — it appears as an advisory in the listing (per the brief's expectation). Mozilla Observatory still **A+, 110/100, tests_passed 10/10, tests_failed 0** (verified via `https://observatory-api.mdn.mozilla.net/api/v2/scan` POST API; new scan id 93649143, scanned_at 2026-04-27T18:46:30Z). No regression from Phase 7B-2 baseline. **PageSpeed Insights post-deploy numbers:** Google's unauthenticated PageSpeed API was again rate-limited at commit time (`429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` — the daily quota for the unauth project is shared and was not yet reset from the Phase 7B-2 attempt). The user's manual web-UI run will confirm the SEO 92→100 lift (descriptive-link finding clears) and the Best Practices unscored COOP advisory clears; Performance and Accessibility expected unchanged within the standard run-to-run variance band. Document the resulting numbers when the manual run is done. **Constraint compliance:** zero new packages; zero new files; three existing files modified (`src/lib/constants.ts` one-line copy edit, `next.config.ts` one-line header addition, `src/lib/security-headers.ts` one-line array extension plus a named const + comment block); no other content edits, no Tailwind/theme/component changes; PROJECT_MAP regen NOT triggered (no new files, no deletions, no renames — per the brief's explicit skip directive). **Deviations from the prompt with reasons:** (a) the brief said the helper extension was a "Recommendation: add to the helper. Final call is the audit's after reading the helper's existing scope." The audit confirmed the helper follows a clean `[key, value]` tuple pattern with a single appendable array, and the cost of one additional entry is minimal — extension applied. No deviation from intent. (b) PageSpeed before/after numbers are not in this row because the unauthenticated PageSpeed API was rate-limited at commit time (same constraint that hit Phase 7B-2's PageSpeed write-up); user's web-UI manual run will close the loop. (c) No architectural-notes update — COOP is a static header, the helper extension follows the existing pattern, and there is no new framework-behaviour finding worth adding to the Phase 7B-1 / 7B-2 architecture-notes section. **Roll-back plan:** if any post-deploy regression appears (extremely unlikely given the value choice), revert the `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy` entry from `next.config.ts:headers()` and the `SECURITY_HEADERS` array in `security-headers.ts`. The CTA copy revert is one string in `constants.ts`. Each is independently revertable. | COMPLETE 2026-04-27 |

Section header is an h2 "System requirements" with subtitle "InnerZero scales from modest laptops to dedicated workstations. Pick a tier. Zero detects your hardware and configures itself on first launch." No header, footer, or mobile-nav touch. No body changes to `/pricing`, `/privacy`, `/terms`, `/models`, `/what-is-local-ai`, `/for/*`, or `/blog/*`. | **Blog scheduling: Front Matter CMS install + date-based queue** | Replaced NN-prefix queue ordering with frontmatter `date:` field so the queue is visually schedulable in Front Matter CMS (free VS Code extension, `eliostruyf.vscode-front-matter`). New `frontmatter.json` at repo root registers both `src/content/blog/` ("Published") and `blog-queue/` ("Scheduled") as page folders under a single `blog` content type matching the existing schema (title, description, date, tags, slug, readingTime, optional featured + draft). `filePrefix: null` on both folders stops the date-prefix auto-prepend that would conflict with slug-only filenames. `.gitignore` updated for `.frontmatter/database/` and `.frontmatter/content/` local state. `scripts/publish-next-blog-post.py` rewritten: iterates every `.md` in `blog-queue/`, picks the file with the earliest frontmatter `date <=` today UTC (filename alphabetical tie-break), no longer strips an `NN-` prefix or substitutes today's-date into a placeholder. Uses an optional `python-frontmatter` import path with a regex fallback for CI (the workflow does not pip-install anything). Defensive `_normalise_text` helper strips a leading UTF-8 BOM and normalises CRLF to LF at every read site so the regex parser cannot silently fail on a Windows-saved or BOM-prepended queue file. Skips bad-frontmatter files with a warning instead of aborting (one malformed file no longer blocks a good one). Empty/no-candidate path exits 0 cleanly, the workflow's empty-push guard handles it. All existing guards retained: target-path collision exit 1, git failure exit 1, `DRY_RUN` honoured. Migration step 4 said "Leave the body untouched" so the body wasn't edited; instead the new publish script substitutes any remaining body PUBLISH_DATE_PLACEHOLDER tokens with the frontmatter's date value at publish time, so the live `.mdx` is well-formed. The substitution is logged with a count and warned if any token reaches the frontmatter (defensive — should never happen in normal use). **Cron unchanged** (`.github/workflows/blog-auto-publish.yml` still fires Tuesday 09:00 UTC and just calls the script; verified untouched via `git status`). **Customer-facing site behaviour unchanged**: `src/lib/blog.ts`, feed routes, JSON-LD, sitemap, blog rendering, and the published `.mdx` files all byte-identical. **Validation:** `python scripts/publish-next-blog-post.py` with `DRY_RUN=1` correctly reports "queue empty or all future-dated, exiting cleanly" today (3 May 2026 Sunday) since the soonest-dated post (2026-05-05) is still future. `npm run build` clean. | COMPLETE 2026-05-03 |

| **FAQPage JSON-LD on blog posts** | Added a second `@type: FAQPage` JSON-LD block to `src/app/(marketing)/blog/[slug]/page.tsx`, emitted only on posts whose MDX body contains a `## Frequently asked questions` section. Existing `BlogPosting` block unchanged. New helper `src/lib/blog-faq.ts` exports a single pure `extractFaqs(body: string): FaqEntry[]` function: locates the FAQ section by case-insensitive h2 match (`/^##\s+frequently asked questions\s*$/im`), walks h3 questions until the next h2 or EOF, strips markdown formatting from answer text (bold, italic, links resolve to anchor text only with URL dropped, inline code, blank-line collapse to single space), normalises trailing question marks, and returns plain-text Q&A pairs. Defensive guards: empty/oversized question (>300 chars) or answer (>2000 chars) rejects the pair, missing FAQ section returns `[]`, no fs or network side effects. **Anti-hallucination discipline:** answer text comes verbatim from the post body, no LLM rewriting; if the body says "yes, but X" the JSON-LD says "yes, but X". Smoke-tested against the full corpus via temporary `scratch/faq-parser-smoke.mjs` (deleted before commit; `scratch/` had to be created for this run since it was not pre-existing): 18 of 43 .mdx + .md files emit FAQPage blocks (3 published + 15 queued), 92 total Q&A pairs extracted, avg 5.1 per FAQ post. Manual h3 count cross-check: parser output matched hand-counted h3 totals on 3 spot-checked files (`innerzero-vs-cursor.mdx`: 5/5, `best-privacy-focused-ai-chatbot-self-hosted.md`: 5/5, `best-local-private-voice-ai-assistant-2026.md`: 6/6). Markdown-leak check (regex `(\*\*\|`\|\]\()` against extracted answers): zero leaks across all spot-checked pairs. **Live render verified** via `npm run start` + curl: `/blog/innerzero-vs-cursor` (FAQ-bearing) renders both `BlogPosting` and `FAQPage` JSON-LD blocks (one of each); `/blog/ai-that-remembers` (no FAQ) renders only `BlogPosting`. Sampled FAQPage payload confirms expected schema shape (`@context` + `@type: "FAQPage"` + `mainEntity` array of `Question` objects each carrying `name` + nested `acceptedAnswer.text`). **Scope guards held:** zero changes to MDX frontmatter schema, post content, `src/lib/blog.ts` signatures, sitemap, feeds, or visible UI; no new npm packages; matched the existing raw `<script type="application/ld+json">` pattern rather than the `JsonLd` component (the existing `BlogPosting` block uses raw script tag, minimum-change rule). **Pattern note:** the page reads `post.content` from `getPostBySlug` (the markdown body after gray-matter strip), passed to `extractFaqs`. Field name on `BlogPost` is `content`, not `body` (the prompt's `body` parameter name is just the helper's argument name). **Validation:** `npm run build` clean, 53 routes, 2 pre-existing warnings (turbopack.root + middleware-to-proxy deprecation). All 8 Batch 1 + Batch 2 queue posts already use the standard FAQ heading shape, so they will emit FAQPage automatically when they publish via the existing cron. | COMPLETE 2026-05-03 |
| **Local blog scheduler UI (offline tool)** | New developer-facing tool at `tools/blog-scheduler/` providing a Lexend-styled month-grid calendar view of every blog post in the repo, colour-coded by status (published green, scheduled orange, overdue red, error red-hatched, draft grey). `server.py` is a Python stdlib-only HTTP server (no new pip deps; uses `http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer` + a custom handler) bound to `127.0.0.1:7878`; on every `/api/posts` request it walks `src/content/blog/*.mdx` and `blog-queue/*.md`, parses YAML frontmatter via a hand-rolled regex parser handling the same quoted-string + inline-list + bool subset the publish script handles (BOM strip + CRLF normalisation reused verbatim from `scripts/publish-next-blog-post.py::_normalise_text`), and computes per-post status: `published` for `src/content/blog/`, `scheduled` for queue files dated today-or-future, `overdue` for queue files dated past, `error` for malformed frontmatter or duplicate slugs, `draft` for `draft: true`. Each post in the response carries `live_url` (`https://innerzero.com/blog/<slug>`) and `vscode_url` (`vscode://file/<forward-slashed-abs-path>` for direct editing). Slug-collision pass flags both involved posts; published posts retain their `published` status but get the error annotation. `index.html` is a single self-contained file with vanilla JS (no framework, no CDN) rendering a 7-column ISO-week-Monday-start calendar grid; clicking a published chip opens the live URL in a new tab, clicking a queued chip opens VS Code via the `vscode://file/` scheme. Side panel slides in on hover/focus and shows full post details with badges, tag pills, and an error list when relevant. Auto-refreshes on window focus so editing a queue file updates the calendar without manual refresh. Dark theme default with light-mode toggle persisted to `localStorage`. Status icons are inline SVGs (no emojis per project rule); status colour AND a glyph (so colour is not the only signal, accessibility). `launch.bat` opens the browser via `start ""` then runs `python server.py` attached to its own console window; closing the launcher window stops the server cleanly. **Scope guards held:** zero changes to `src/content/blog/`, `blog-queue/`, `src/lib/blog.ts`, the publish script, the cron workflow, or any rendered website behaviour. Read-only viewer; no editing path through this UI. Localhost bind only (`HOST = "127.0.0.1"` is hardcoded; never `0.0.0.0`). No npm or pip dependencies added. **Validation:** server smoke test confirmed `stats: {published: 28, scheduled: 15, overdue: 0, errors: 0, draft: 0}` (corpus has 28 published posts vs the prompt's "25 or current" sanity expectation; 15 scheduled matches exactly), sample published post (`ai-that-remembers`) and sample scheduled post (`ai-coding-agent-sandbox-safety`) both emit correct fields including the live URL and the forward-slashed `vscode://file/` URL; `GET /` returns `200 text/html` with the index payload (24,663 bytes); `GET /nonexistent` returns 404. `npm run build` clean, 53 routes, 2 pre-existing warnings (turbopack.root + middleware-to-proxy deprecation). Manual launcher test deferred to Louie (requires a real Windows desktop session, not a Claude Code shell). **Folded in:** `/scratch/` added to `.gitignore` (was outstanding from the FAQPage phase). | COMPLETE 2026-05-03 |
| **Blog scheduler UI v2: publish time, layout fix, filters, search, keyboard shortcuts** | Iteration on the local blog scheduler tool. **Side panel layout fixed:** switched from absolute overlay to a flexbox row layout where the calendar takes `flex: 1 1 auto; min-width: 0` and the panel takes `flex: 0 0 360px` when open and `flex: 0 0 0; opacity: 0` when collapsed. The rightmost calendar column is now always visible and clickable; the calendar shrinks to make room for the panel rather than being hidden behind it. **Pinned vs preview state machine added:** chip hover/focus opens a preview panel; mouseleave on both chip and panel triggers a 200ms close timer; chip click pins the panel until the X button, Esc key, or click on another chip. Hovering the panel itself cancels the close timer so the user can move the cursor into the panel without losing it. The "Open live URL" and "Edit in VS Code" buttons leave the panel open after firing, so the user can dip into a post and come back. **Click semantics changed:** the previous v1 chip-click-opens-target behaviour is gone; click now ALWAYS pins the panel and the user navigates explicitly via the panel buttons. **Publish time displayed:** server.py adds `CRON_EXPRESSION = "0 9 * * 2,5"` and `PUBLISH_TIME_UTC = "09:00"` constants; the `/api/posts` JSON now carries `cron_expression` + `publish_time_utc` at the top level and `publish_time_utc` per post. Chips show "09:00" appended to the title in 11px muted text on the same line. Side panel shows a "PUBLISH TIME" row with "09:00 UTC (HH:MM your local time)" computed via `Intl.DateTimeFormat({hour, minute, hour12: false, timeZoneName: "short"})`; if `timeZoneName` is unsupported on the runtime, falls back to omitting the zone abbreviation. **Filter pills:** 5 toggle pills (Published, Scheduled, Overdue, Errors, Drafts) above the calendar; ARIA-pressed semantics; default all-on; state persisted to `localStorage` under `blog-scheduler-filters-v2`. Posts with hidden statuses are removed from the calendar entirely (cleaner than dimming). **Search box:** filters by case-insensitive substring across title, slug, and tags; "(showing X of Y)" indicator appears in the stats line whenever a filter or search is active; clear-X button hidden when empty; session-only state (intentionally not persisted). **Tag pills clickable:** clicking a tag in the side panel sets the search box to the tag's text (so the natural substring search picks it up); clicking the same tag again clears it; the active tag pill highlights with the accent colour. **Keyboard shortcuts** wired via a single `keydown` handler at document level: ←/→ for prev/next month, T for today, / focuses the search input, Esc closes panel-then-clears-search-then-blurs-focus in priority order, ? toggles a centred help overlay. Shortcuts that aren't `/`/Esc are ignored when an `<input>`/`<textarea>`/contenteditable is focused so typing in the search box does not trigger navigation. The help overlay also reachable via a "?" icon button top-right. **Scope guards held:** zero changes to `src/`, `blog-queue/`, publish script, cron workflow, or any rendered website behaviour. No new pip or npm dependencies. Same Python stdlib HTTP server, same vanilla-JS frontend (no framework, no CDN). README updated with new filters + shortcuts sections. **Validation:** server smoke confirmed `cron_expression: "0 9 * * 2,5"`, top-level `publish_time_utc: "09:00"`, every post (43 of 43) carries `publish_time_utc: "09:00"`; stats unchanged (28 published / 15 scheduled / 0 overdue / 0 errors). `npm run build` clean: 53 routes, 0 errors, 2 pre-existing warnings (turbopack.root + middleware-to-proxy). Manual UI verification deferred to Louie. **Week view (Step 10 in the prompt) deferred** as a future enhancement: the dual-mode arrow-key navigation (week-by-week in week view vs month-by-month in month view) plus week-anchor state plus the smooth transition between modes would push past the ~30 min budget the prompt allowed for it. Documented as a future feature; the other 9 features all landed. **Smoke incident logged:** the first smoke test returned `cron_expression: None` because two stale `python server.py` processes from earlier phase smokes were both still LISTENING on port 7878, and the new process bound to a second listener slot. Killed both PIDs explicitly via `taskkill /F`, restarted cleanly, smoke passed. Future tools that bind to fixed ports for testing should `taskkill` the prior listener up front rather than assume a clean port. | COMPLETE 2026-05-03 |
| **Blog scheduler v2.1: simplify panel to always-on with empty-state link board** | Reverted v2's preview/pinned/auto-close panel state machine after the close-on-mouseout transition was disorienting in practice. New model: panel is permanently visible at 360 px on the right, never animates, never collapses. Three content states only: empty (default link board on first load), hover (chip mouseenter sets content; mouseleave does nothing, panel stays), selected (chip click adds an outline ring + sets content; click another chip moves the ring). Single piece of UI state on the JS side: `currentPanelPostSlug` string-or-null; null renders the empty state, anything else renders that post. Empty-state link board lists 18 useful URLs grouped by Site / Repo / Community / Tools / Desktop repo, mixing https external links (open in new tab) with `vscode://file/...` local-folder shortcuts (browser hands off to the OS, page stays put). Post-audit at .ai/audits/post/title_duplicate_brand_fix_post_audit.md. Surfaced from GSC investigation: pricing 0.8% CTR at pos 2.7, download 6.7% at pos 2.3, blog 0% at pos 3.5, likely primary cause of CTR underperformance since Google often rewrites SERP titles when source HTML contains duplicated brand strings. | **SEO: JSON Feed relocate off robots-blocked /api/* (GSC "Indexed, though blocked by robots.txt")** | Google Search Console flagged `https://innerzero.com/api/feed` as "Indexed, though blocked by robots.txt" (first detected 06/06/2026, 1 page, non-critical). Root cause: `public/robots.txt` has `Disallow: /api/*`, but the JSON Feed handler lived at `src/app/api/feed/route.ts` (served `/api/feed`) and was advertised to crawlers by the `<link rel="alternate" type="application/feed+json">` in `layout.tsx` plus the footer JSON link, so Googlebot discovered the URL it was forbidden to crawl. The two RSS feeds (`/feed.xml`, `/changelog.xml`) already sat at clean top-level paths, allowed by robots and excluded from middleware; the JSON feed was the lone inconsistency. **Fix (LOW risk, routing/SEO only, zero deps, no Supabase/Stripe/RLS/CSP/env change):** (1) relocated the handler `src/app/api/feed/route.ts` -> `src/app/feed.json/route.ts` (served `/feed.json`, byte-identical logic, only the self `feed_url` string changed from `${SITE_URL}/api/feed` to `${SITE_URL}/feed.json`); (2) `layout.tsx` + `Footer.tsx` discovery links repointed `/api/feed` -> `/feed.json`; (3) `src/middleware.ts` matcher adds `feed\.json` to the exclusion alternation next to `feed\.xml` / `changelog\.xml` (+ comment); (4) `next.config.ts` `redirects()` adds a permanent 308 `/api/feed` -> `/feed.json` so existing feed-reader subscribers keep working; (5) `public/robots.txt` adds `Allow: /api/feed` BEFORE `Disallow: /api/*` (longest-match precedence re-allows only that one path) so Googlebot can crawl the old URL, follow the 308, and drop the stale blocked-but-indexed entry. Every `/api/*` path other than `/api/feed` stays blocked. **Consistency:** all three feeds now top-level (`/feed.xml`, `/feed.json`, `/changelog.xml`), all excluded from middleware, all advertised via top-level links, all `○ (Static)` in the build. sitemap.ts (feeds intentionally not sitemap URLs), llms.txt/llms-full.txt (reference `/feed.xml`, never `/api/feed`) verified unaffected. **Validation:** `npm run lint` at unchanged baseline (6 err + 6 warn, all pre-existing in Turnstile/spending-cap/supabase-middleware; zero of my 5 files appear); `npm run build` clean after `.next` clear (only the 2 pre-existing turbopack.root + middleware-to-proxy warnings); `/feed.json` confirmed in route list as Static; `/api/feed` gone from route table. Live `next start` curls: `/feed.json` -> 200 `application/feed+json`, `feed_url` `https://innerzero.com/feed.json`, 57 items, security headers intact (COOP/X-Frame via next.config); `/api/feed` -> 308 -> `/feed.json` -> 200; `/feed.xml` + `/changelog.xml` -> 200 unchanged; robots served with `Allow: /api/feed` above `Disallow`. 6 files changed +26/-5, 1 file deleted (-100), 1 file added. Packet at `WEBCLAUDE.local.md`. **Operator action after deploy:** in GSC open the "Indexed, though blocked by robots.txt" issue and click VALIDATE FIX; GSC clears over days as Google re-crawls. | COMPLETE 2026-07-09 |

## PROJECT_MAP.md maintenance rule

PROJECT_MAP.md at `C:\Users\sumlu\Documents\InnerZero\PROJECT_MAP.md` is the authoritative path-and-folder index for both the InnerZero desktop repo and the innerzero_website repo. It is auto-generated by `scripts/generate_project_map.py` in the InnerZero repo.

When Claude Code adds, deletes, moves, or renames any file or folder inside either repo, it must re-run the generator at the end of the phase:

```
python C:/Users/sumlu/Documents/InnerZero/scripts/generate_project_map.py
```

This regenerates PROJECT_MAP.md with a fresh "Generated: YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS" timestamp and updated file listing. Stage and commit the regenerated PROJECT_MAP.md alongside the phase commit.

Run the generator after these events, not before:
- New files or folders created
- Existing files or folders deleted
- Files moved between folders
- Files renamed

Do NOT run the generator for:
- Edits to existing files (contents change but paths stay the same)
- Pure documentation or comment changes

If the generator fails for any reason (network, permissions), note it in the commit message but do not block the phase.

## Architecture notes (load-bearing for future header phases)

### Headers from `next.config.ts:headers()` are inherited by manually-constructed `NextResponse` objects

Empirically established in the Phase 7A pre-fix curl audit (2026-04-27, Next.js 16.2.1 + Vercel runtime). Every API route that returns `new NextResponse(..., { headers: { ... } })` or `new Response(..., { headers: { ... } })` with a custom headers literal still emitted the global `next.config.ts:headers()` set on the wire — including `/api/feed` (`new Response`), the `/api/cloud/balance` 401 (`NextResponse.json`), `/api/cloud/plans` (with explicit CORS headers), the OPTIONS 204 preflight, and `/account`'s 307 redirect. The Phase 6 → Phase 7A hypothesis that custom headers bypass `next.config.ts:headers()` was empirically false in this environment.

The ONE response surface the audit found that does NOT inherit `next.config.ts:headers()` is the Vercel-edge HTTP→HTTPS 308 redirect at `http://innerzero.com/` — that response is generated below the Next.js application layer and is unreachable from `next.config.ts`. HSTS preload propagation is the practical mitigation there.

**Why this matters for the helper.** `src/lib/security-headers.ts::applySecurityHeaders` was kept as defence-in-depth for the three routes that build their own response with a custom headers literal, despite the audit showing inheritance works. Its `if (!response.headers.has(key))` guard composes cleanly with the framework's inheritance: when `next.config.ts` already set the header, the helper is a no-op; if a future Next.js change ever breaks inheritance, the helper still backstops those three routes. Phase 7B-1 extended the same helper for `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` and `Report-To` on the same justification.

**Re-verify this assumption when bumping Next.js major.** Inheritance is not in any documented contract — it's framework behaviour at the runtime layer. A Next.js 17 / 18 upgrade should re-run the Phase 7A curl sweep against the same paths and confirm before assuming the helper remains a no-op.

### Next.js 16.2.1 nonce auto-propagation behaviour

Empirically established in the Phase 7B-2 post-deploy smoke (2026-04-27, Next.js 16.2.1 + Vercel Edge runtime). The contract documented at `/vercel/next.js` Content Security Policy guide holds: when middleware sets `x-nonce` AND the `Content-Security-Policy` value on the FORWARDED REQUEST headers (via `NextResponse.next({ request: { headers: requestHeaders } })`), Next.js parses the request's CSP header at SSR time, extracts the nonce via the `'nonce-{value}'` pattern, and applies the `nonce` attribute automatically to:

- React framework scripts and the Next.js runtime loader
- Per-page JavaScript bundles
- Streaming hydration inline `<script>` tags (the `self.__next_f.push(...)` chunks that were the Phase 7B-1 blocker)
- `<Script>` components rendered with `nonce={nonce}` prop

Verified post-deploy on `/` (28/28 inline scripts nonce-tagged, zero without), `/blog/innerzero-is-live` (15/15 nonce-tagged), and across every page the smoke covered. **No manual `<Script nonce={…}>` wiring is required for Next.js's own emitted scripts** — the existing call sites in `src/app/layout.tsx` for the theme-flash IIFE add the nonce attribute manually only as defence-in-depth alongside the SHA-256 hash.

**Strict-dynamic propagation to dynamically-injected scripts works.** Cloudflare Turnstile (loaded via `document.createElement('script')` in `src/components/ui/Turnstile.tsx`) and Vercel Analytics (auto-injected by `@vercel/analytics/react`) both successfully fetch and execute under `script-src 'self' 'nonce-X' 'strict-dynamic' '<theme-hash>'` with NO host allowlists in `script-src`. The chain is: trusted React runtime (nonce-tagged) → component code that calls `createElement('script')` → the new script inherits trust via `'strict-dynamic'`. This was the gating uncertainty for Phase 7B-2 and the fact it works in Next.js 16.2.1 is what enabled the enforce flip without `'unsafe-inline'`.

**Dynamic-rendering opt-in.** `await headers()` (or `cookies()`, `searchParams`) in any component on the path — root layout in our case — is what flips a route from `○ (Static)` to `ƒ (Dynamic)`. Without the dynamic API call, the page is statically rendered at build time and Next.js never gets to read the per-request CSP / x-nonce. The route-table diff in `npm run build` is the verification: 24 marketing/auth/blog routes flipped to `ƒ` after the layout edit, confirming the cascade is automatic.

**Re-verify on Next.js major bumps.** Auto-propagation is documented but not in any stable contract. A Next.js 17 / 18 / 19 upgrade should re-run the Phase 7B-2 smoke (count of nonce-tagged inline scripts on `/`, Turnstile load on `/login`, Vercel Analytics resources on `/`) before assuming the chain still composes.
